ABSENT DIRECTIVES — named in architecture, in no ROADMAP row:
  03-ir.md:132  [Intermediate representation > The base map: fully-decomposed primitive DAG, permanently retained] **Pattern harvesting against canonical form** (see [08-pattern-harvest](08-pattern-harvest.md)) — reading the base map avoids biasing toward optimizations that already fired.
  03-ir.md:133  [Intermediate representation > The base map: fully-decomposed primitive DAG, permanently retained] **Bisection during debugging** — compare an optimized run against the base map's strict execution to isolate a divergence.
  03-ir.md:174  [Intermediate representation > Persisting the unified graph (base map + optimized paths)] **A stripper tool removes everything but the base map** for clean distribution (smallest portable artifact). It is a convenience, not a requirement — the model runs on any machine either way.
  04-optimization.md:41  [Optimization > DecompositionMap > `decompose` is total, never-panics, and is the base map's fixpoint] A `panic!` in `decompose` is **always a bug**: the op is either a true primitive (must return self) or a non-primitive whose recipe is missing (a bug / basis gap). The two are distinguished by **basis membership, never by the return value**. A non-basis op that fails to decompose is a **surfaced opaque-op gap** — flagged on the base map and fed to the missing-fusion / inventory telemetry ([08-pattern-harvest](08-pattern-harvest.md)) — never a crash and never silently masquerading as a primitive.
  04-optimization.md:65  [Optimization > OptimizationMap] **Cost contribution**: how the optimizer should score a route that includes this rule's rewrite. Static annotation (FLOPs delta, bytes-moved delta, kernel-overhead delta) plus error contribution (for tolerance gating). Refined by empirical Judge data over time.
  04-optimization.md:67  [Optimization > OptimizationMap] `Concurrent` — the rule's matches are local enough to fit within the optimizer's lookahead window. The rule never needs to rewrite a node that's already been finalized. Safe under concurrent execution.
  04-optimization.md:68  [Optimization > OptimizationMap] `WholeGraph` — the rule's matches may span arbitrary distances and can target nodes whose finalization status varies. Common for rules like cross-graph CSE, global re-association, or whole-program dead-code elimination. Cannot fire under concurrent execution; must run before the optimization frontier starts advancing.
  04-optimization.md:100  [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > Storage and structural-distinctness] Alternatives are paths in the multi-path graph, not copies of whole graphs; shared regions are shared. Total storage scales with the bounded per-device frontier (order 10² paths), not with N complete graph copies — well within budget for typical inference graphs.
  04-optimization.md:101  [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > Storage and structural-distinctness] Subgraphs not at decision points are shared — they're regions where the optimizer found exactly one viable alternative.
  04-optimization.md:103  [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > Storage and structural-distinctness] What is *not* enforced is meaningful diversity beyond structural distinctness: two distinct alternatives might differ only trivially (same kernel choices, same placements, slightly different intermediate node ordering) and so present the runtime picker with redundant options. Future refinement could enforce explicit diversity (e.g., "the N alternatives at a decision point must differ on at least one placement or one tolerance trade-off") if shallow distinctness turns out to be common in practice.
  04-optimization.md:129  [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > What makes an alternative admissible] It must satisfy all hard constraints: tolerance budget not exceeded along any path through it, viable backend assignment, all transfers feasible, all layouts reachable via fixup ops.
  04-optimization.md:130  [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > What makes an alternative admissible] Among admissible alternatives, survival is decided **globally** by the per-device Pareto frontier + crowding cap (see [Bounding the frontier](#bounding-the-frontier-pareto-per-device--crowding-cap)), not by a fixed per-branch count. How many alternatives a single branch shows is *emergent* — whatever the surviving paths imply there — not a separately-tuned N.
  04-optimization.md:151  [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] **`WholeGraph`-classified rules disable concurrency wherever they fire.** A decision-point alternative produced by a `WholeGraph` rule cannot be picked under concurrent execute. The optimizer must complete optimization of the whole graph before execution starts when any `WholeGraph` rule has produced an alternative the picker might use. Such alternatives are marked `concurrent-incompatible`; the runtime picker treats them accordingly.
  04-optimization.md:153  [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] **Per-decision-point commit at the frontier.** As the frontier passes a decision point that's still ahead of execution, the optimizer must commit: one of the concurrent-compatible alternatives wins for that location. The runtime picker is constrained to the committed alternative from there forward. (Decision points whose alternatives all require `WholeGraph` rules don't participate in concurrent execute at all; the picker chooses among them only after the whole graph is optimized.) This means the per-decision-point alternative set can shrink to one as the frontier passes, never grow.
  04-optimization.md:158  [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] `Concurrency::Required` — only concurrent-compatible alternatives are eligible at every decision point. If a decision point has no concurrent-compatible alternative, the realize fails or surfaces an error.
  04-optimization.md:159  [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] `Concurrency::Forbidden` — only whole-graph alternatives are eligible. Useful for one-shot batch computations where the latency-to-first-output benefit doesn't justify the complexity.
  04-optimization.md:168  [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] **Implementation complexity is high.** Frontier tracking, top-N collapse mechanics, atomic-swap of route assignments at the frontier — each is non-trivial. Race conditions are subtle. Tests and invariants must be airtight.
  04-optimization.md:239  [Optimization > Load-time incremental planning + cost-based placement (v0.4) > Cost-based placement: the carry-forward DP] **Commit**: choices finalize only at the execution frontier
  04-optimization.md:262  [Optimization > Load-time incremental planning + cost-based placement (v0.4) > Plan-fragment memoization (repeated structure)] **Persisted-plan cache** fragments (per
  04-optimization.md:266  [Optimization > Load-time incremental planning + cost-based placement (v0.4) > Plan-fragment memoization (repeated structure)] **Record-once-replay execution** — a repeated fragment is the
  04-optimization.md:271  [Optimization > Load-time incremental planning + cost-based placement (v0.4) > Plan-fragment memoization (repeated structure)] **Activation-pool sizing** — identical fragments have identical
  04-optimization.md:332  [Optimization > Tolerance budgets gate which rules fire] Each rule declares its error contribution (zero for strict-equivalence rewrites, non-zero for approximate ones).
  04-optimization.md:333  [Optimization > Tolerance budgets gate which rules fire] The optimizer tracks cumulative error along each candidate route's path through the graph. Cumulative tracking respects the rule's compositional behavior (additive for most ops, multiplicative for some, nonlinear for a few; details in 07).
  04-optimization.md:334  [Optimization > Tolerance budgets gate which rules fire] A rule may not fire on a node if the resulting cumulative error along *any* output path through that node would exceed the budget.
  04-optimization.md:335  [Optimization > Tolerance budgets gate which rules fire] Routes with zero non-strict rewrites are always admissible (under any non-negative tolerance). Routes with non-zero rewrites are admissible only under the corresponding tolerance budget.
  06-runtime.md:24  [Runtime > The runtime's responsibilities] **Cache management** — [11-persistence](11-persistence.md)'s. The runtime loads the persisted graph and writes refined plans, but doesn't decide cache policy.
  06-runtime.md:81  [Runtime > Data parallelism: independent runs run concurrently] Across backends, multiple runs are simultaneously in flight; within one backend, multiple slots dispatch to the device's parallelism primitives (CUDA streams, Vulkan queues, CPU sub-pools).
  06-runtime.md:119  [Runtime > Background re-optimization] The optimizer walks decision points (topological order).
  06-runtime.md:120  [Runtime > Background re-optimization] At each: take the union of the loaded paths and any the local optimizer can produce with local empirical data; re-rank by local cost; converge structurally-equivalent paths; **keep the per-device Pareto frontier (crowding-capped)**; atomic-swap that decision point's path set in place.

  10-decisions-log.md:305  [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] **Load vs import.** `map_from_file()` (or similar) LOADS a finalized native Fuel graph+storage (mmap'd). `from_gguf()`/`from_safetensors()`/HF etc. are IMPORTS (conversion → base map). The native `.fuel` format (L1) is the load path; everything else converts.
  10-decisions-log.md:307  [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] **Storage classes via "session index."** A node's lifetime/sharing class is inferred from its op (`Op::Const` → shared/weights; cache-write target → session-state with explicit override; else → transient), with multiple storages per node indexed by **session** for session-state. Weights are shared across sessions (not session-indexed). Transient never persists and never crosses to the file, but DOES cross devices mid-realize (D2D) on multi-device paths. Shared written-back only if changed (training); session written-back only on snapshot.
  10-decisions-log.md:312  [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] **`Storage` must support mmap-backed zero-copy views** (paged), not only owned buffers — prerequisite for larger-than-RAM and the native format. Today's eager-copy load defeats this and is to be replaced.
  10-decisions-log.md:334  [Decisions log > 2026-06-20 — Adaptive runtime fusion: the recipe principle, two-tier extensibility, and the Fuel-strategist / backend-synthesizer JIT loop] **`decompose` is TOTAL + never-panic + primitive→self (G2).** `decompose` never `panic!`s (the never-panic constitution rule). A **primitive decomposes to itself** — the recursion's fixpoint, already the identity form `decompose = |_g, id, _p| id` at `fuel-graph/src/registry.rs:823`. The **base map is the fixpoint of `decompose` over every node** (lower until `decompose(x) == x`; a primitive is just a node no lowering rule fires on — exactly the `optimize_graph` rewrite model). A panicking `decompose` is **always a bug**: the op is either a true primitive (must return self) or a non-primitive missing its recipe (a bug / basis gap), distinguished by **basis membership, never by the return value**; a non-basis op that fails to decompose is a **surfaced opaque-op gap** (a base-map flag → the missing-fusion / inventory telemetry), never a crash and never silently masquerading as primitive. This is **load-bearing for optimization *itself***, not just JIT: optimization = lower-to-base-map + find-best-cover, so an op that won't decompose *breaks the optimizer*, not merely a downstream feature. The three current panicking decomposes (`nf4_matmul.rs:120`, `flash_attn`, `selective_scan`) are bugs to fix, not a permanent category. A model's recipe (`decompose` + `pattern`) **always ships with the fused op** — never deferred "until intermediates fit" (that produces an opaque island).
  10-decisions-log.md:336  [Decisions log > 2026-06-20 — Adaptive runtime fusion: the recipe principle, two-tier extensibility, and the Fuel-strategist / backend-synthesizer JIT loop] **The primitive basis is build-time-closed (G3).** The primitive `Op` set is a compile-time Rust enum (`fuel-graph/src/lib.rs`); there is **no generic opaque / `Custom` node** in the lazy graph. So an externally-supplied (provider / JIT) op **cannot become a new primitive at runtime**. It must either **decompose into the existing primitive basis** (and carry a `pattern` to replace that sequence), or — if it needs a primitive Fuel lacks (e.g. a higher-order `Scan` for SSMs) — prompt a **Fuel-side, build-time `Op`-enum extension** the provider cannot do itself. The primitive vocabulary is a **hard shared contract** with providers. JIT adds kernels / recipes *over* existing primitives, never new primitives.
  10-decisions-log.md:444  [Decisions log > 2026-07-04 — FKC reaches all three backends; contracts can pin a cost fn (trampoline); optimize-time kernel-variant selection reads the Judge] **Same-device kernel-variant selection is baked at optimize time, reading the Judge.** `variant_bake.rs` collapses a same-device `Op::Branch` (e.g. the decomposed attention region vs. a CUDA flash arm) to the cheaper arm at optimize time — the runtime route picker resolves *placement* arms only (keyed on arm 0's op), so a same-device kernel-variant choice must be baked, per 04-optimization ("baked at optimize time"). The bake reads **measured** latency (`decode_arm_composite_ns_judged`, Layer-2-first + Layer-1 fallback) so a fused arm that *ties* on Layer-1 FLOPs but wins on measured latency (no materialized attention matrix, one launch) is selected; ties / unknown / capability-missing keep arm 0 (the oracle), so the no-Judge path is byte-identical. A Judge *measurement is capability evidence* (the cell exists only because the kernel ran during profiling), so a measured cell is admissible past the Layer-1 availability gate.
  10-decisions-log.md:446  [Decisions log > 2026-07-04 — FKC reaches all three backends; contracts can pin a cost fn (trampoline); optimize-time kernel-variant selection reads the Judge] **The Judge gained decode-shape Layer-2 coverage**, which the bake above consumes: f16/bf16 dtypes, decode-shaped ladders (skinny GEMV + softmax rows), and `OpKind::FlashAttn` as a profiled op (`SizeClass::attention`). This surfaced and fixed **a latent correctness bug**: `SizeClass` was an aspect-blind `log2(total_elements)` and the Judge *producer* keyed a matmul on `m·n` (output) while the ranker *consumer* keyed on `m·k` (LHS input) — square matmuls agreed by accident, **every non-square matmul in every model had an unreachable/poisoned Judge cell**. Fixed via one shared `SizeClass::matmul(m,n,k)` aspect key both sides derive (`SizeClass::for_op`), `PROFILE_REPORT_VERSION` 2→4.
  10-decisions-log.md:480  [Decisions log > 2026-07-08 — Binding key generalized; the runtime-kernel sidecar is FOLDED] **`default_passes_with_runtime_fusion`** therefore also remains: its hermeticity rationale was always the *metadata* scan (the pathfinder iterates `runtime_entries()`), which is still process-global.
  10-decisions-log.md:746  [Decisions log > 2026-07-14 — KISS conformance pass: four seam/verify latent-bug fixes + adopt-from-KISS goals] **`SeamHello.reserved1`** — the 6 alignment-padding bytes between `profiles` (offset 42) and `capabilities` (offset 48) were implicit `#[repr(C)]` padding Rust neither guarantees zeroed nor lets a reader inspect. Made explicit as a managed `reserved1: [u8; 6]` field: zeroed by `advertise()`, hard-rejected when nonzero by `validate()` (new `SeamError::ReservedNonZero`), offset asserted in the frozen-layout test. Layout-preserving (still 56 bytes).

  10-decisions-log.md:1472  [Decisions log > 2026-08-12 — A decline carries TWO axes: what KIND of token it is, and WHY the capability is absent. They compose as two fields and never merge.] **KIND** — `TokenKind` (`fuel-ir/src/token_kind.rs`): a fact about a **token's membership in the interop vocabulary**. `Supported` / `RecognizedUnsupported` / `Reserved` / `Unknown`, a **total partition** of KISS sk4's 24 tokens (14 + 8 + 2 = 24), asserted against the standard's own list with the `Supported` side **derived** from `DType::ALL` rather than hand-listed.
  10-decisions-log.md:1473  [Decisions log > 2026-08-12 — A decline carries TWO axes: what KIND of token it is, and WHY the capability is absent.   10-decisions-log.md:1496  [Decisions log > 2026-08-12 — A decline carries TWO axes: what KIND of token it is, and WHY the capability is absent. They compose as two fields and never merge.] **A canary must probe on the SAME SIDE as the trigger.** Storage's only expiry trigger is Fuel-side — `CudaStorageSlice` is a Fuel enum, so no external bump can create storage. A Fuel-mirrored assertion is therefore complete *for storage*. Reused for an **externally**-triggered capability it is blind **by construction**: `ElementKind::U32` appeared in a dependency's vocabulary and a Fuel-side list could never have seen it. For those, the instrument is a **wildcard-free match over the external enum**, which becomes a compile error on a dependency bump — available only when that enum is not `#[non_exhaustive]` (`ElementKind` is not; `OpKind` is, and `fkc/lower.rs` already documents that the anchor is impossible there). **Availability is a property of the specific dependency, not a technique.**
  10-decisions-log.md:1544  [Decisions log > 2026-08-15 — The derived-precision program: a bound computed from a verified basis, not declared] **A runner for decompositions.** `KernelInvoker::invoke` takes a `BindingEntry` (one registered kernel); `decompose_via_recipe` mutates a `Graph` and returns a `NodeId`. **A `GraphInvoker` is the missing piece** — and it must route through the fallible `pipelined_bridge::realize_one_as::<T>()` path, never GAP-186's panicking `realize_*` accessors.

  2026-11-07-fireline-fuel-baracuda-seam-plan.md:69  [Fuel half of the FireLine seam plan: the Fuel <-> baracuda (kernel-synthesizer) interface > 8.   03-ir.md:3  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation] matmul role-vector `op_attrs` serialize/resolve live in both directions
  03-ir.md:3  [Named / StatusNote] [Intermediate representation] v0.6 was a MAJOR core-claim change
  03-ir.md:3  [Named / StatusNote] [Intermediate representation] v0.5 was a MAJOR core-claim change
  03-ir.md:3  [Named / StatusNote] [Intermediate representation] v0.4 was a MAJOR core-claim change
  03-ir.md:17  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > The DAG] **The graph is the model, independent of any input.** It is built when a model is loaded or imported — **not** when a forward pass runs — and is ideally fully optimized before the first input arrives. Inputs and per-session state flow *through* the persistent graph (via storage; see below); the graph itself is not rebuilt per input, per token, or per session. "No graph until `forward()` runs" is a defect this architecture corrects.
  03-ir.md:18  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > The DAG] **Immutable nodes after creation.** A node's op, inputs, dtype, and shape are fixed at construction — where "shape" is the node's *static shape expression*: a concrete dim vector, or (for data-dependent ops) a **capacity/upper-bound shape** whose true valid extent is carried at runtime by a separate count, never by mutating the node (see [Data-dependent output shapes](#data-dependent-output-shapes)). An optimizer that wants to change a node inserts a new node and rewrites consumers' edges to the new id. This makes graph rewrites tractable and lock-free reading possible.
  03-ir.md:18  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > The DAG] Immutable nodes after creation.
  03-ir.md:19  [Directive / StatusNote] [Intermediate representation > The DAG] **Append-only arena, with compaction.** Optimization is append-only — rewrites add nodes and rewire consumers; superseded nodes are left orphaned. Because `optimize_graph`'s pathfinders explore many candidate paths the optimizers then discard, the arena accumulates substantial orphaned debris, so **compaction is required, not optional**: a compaction pass drops unreachable nodes, keeping only the base map plus the surviving optimized multi-path graph. It runs at least **before finalize-to-disk** (so the persisted `.fuel` holds the lean result, not exploration debris) and optionally **between optimization rounds** (to bound the in-memory / disk-backed working set during a long search). This is not a *correctness* concern — orphans never change results — but under the heavy-exploration optimizer it is very much a *size* concern, on disk especially (RAM less so: orphaned pages of an mmap'd graph simply never fault in).
  03-ir.md:19  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > The DAG] before finalize-to-disk
  03-ir.md:19  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > The DAG] between optimization rounds
  03-ir.md:20  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > The DAG] **Side-table metadata.** Information derivable from the graph (view-op layouts) or varying independently of node identity (tolerance budgets, storage, per-path kernel assignments, profile data) lives in side tables keyed by NodeId (and, for session state, by `(NodeId, SessionId)`), not on the node. The node is the structural fact; side tables are interpretations and instances.
  03-ir.md:28  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > The op vocabulary] cannot become a new primitive at runtime
  03-ir.md:32  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > The op vocabulary] populated at startup
  03-ir.md:73  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Higher-order primitives: `Op::Scan`] **Encoding.** The recurrence body is carried as the node's own `inputs`, exactly like `Op::Branch`'s arms: `inputs = [init_carry, xs_0..xs_{n_xs-1}, consts.., body_new_carry, body_y]`. The two body-*exit* NodeIds are always the **last two** inputs — the convention that lets `base_map_hash`'s existing input-recursion hash the body for free, with only a params-only `op_key` arm added for `Op::Scan` itself (over `n_xs`, `bound`, `emit`, and whether `early_exit.is_some()` — never the predicate's identity). Nodes inside the body reference `Op::ScanPlaceholder { role, index }` leaves instead of real operands: `role: Carry` for the per-step carry-in (always `index: 0` — v1 is single-carry), `role: Elem, index: i` for the per-step slice of `xs[i]`. Consts are referenced by ordinary `NodeId` (they don't vary per step). `Op::ScanPlaceholder` is an inert, input-less leaf substituted only by `unroll_scan`; it is never itself realized.
  03-ir.md:76  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Higher-order primitives: `Op::Scan`] **`early_exit` is now a built mechanism (Phase 2).** `Op::Scan.early_exit: Option<ScanPredicate>` carries a convergence predicate; `ScanPredicate` stays a **unit marker** whose `Some`-ness signals "peel one extra trailing input." When `early_exit = Some`, the layout gains exactly one trailing slot: `inputs = [init_carry, xs.., consts.., body_new_carry, body_y, pred_exit]`. `pred_exit` is a scalar-`U8` sub-DAG over the carry (its `Op::ScanPlaceholder{Carry,0}` leaves = the pre-step carry; the shared `body_new_carry` node = the post-step carry, so `‖ξ_new − ξ_old‖ < ε` is expressible as ordinary primitives). Because it is a trailing input, `base_map_hash` / `topo_order_multi` / reachability see it for free (`op_key` folds only its presence into the hash, never the predicate's identity). The `Result`-returning builder `Tensor::scan_until` validates predicate shape/dtype/placeholders at **graph-build time**. Forward realize routes a kernel-less early-exit scan to the host step driver (`drive_scan_until_final_f32`), which stops at the runtime convergence step; `unroll_scan` peels and **ignores** the predicate (the build-time backward/oracle unroll differentiates the full static `bound` — see [04-optimization](04-optimization.md) BPTT).
  03-ir.md:76  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > Higher-order primitives: `Op::Scan`] `early_exit` is now a built mechanism (Phase 2).
  03-ir.md:77  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Higher-order primitives: `Op::Scan`] **A base-map terminal, not an executor-facing op.** No `LoweringRule` matches `Op::Scan` — it IS the primitive, its own fixpoint under `decompose` (see [The base map](#the-base-map-fully-decomposed-primitive-dag-permanently-retained) below). It carries **no native kernel** in Phase 1: the fused SSM ops still dispatch to their existing CPU/CUDA kernels on the executed path, so `Op::Scan` is reached only via explicit lowering — the verify oracle, or a kernel-absent fallback — never by production `realize`. `unroll_scan(graph, scan_id, steps) -> Result<(NodeId, NodeId), fuel_ir::Error>` (`fuel-graph/src/scan.rs`) walks the body once per step, substituting each `Op::ScanPlaceholder` with that step's carry/elem NodeId, and returns `(selected, complementary)` per `emit`. It is never registered as anyone's `decompose` — decomposing a fused op *to* `Op::Scan` is a lowering; unrolling `Op::Scan` further is a separate, opt-in materialization for verification, not part of the base map's own fixpoint.
  03-ir.md:78  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Higher-order primitives: `Op::Scan`] **Payoff is basis closure, not new runtime capability.** Closing G3 means `decompose` is now total over genuine primitives for the whole registered fused-op set (see [08-pattern-harvest](08-pattern-harvest.md)) — the base map has no more SSM-shaped opaque islands, so cost-from-decompose and re-fusion analysis cover it (see [04-optimization](04-optimization.md)). It does **not** mean SSMs run through `Op::Scan` at runtime; the fused kernels remain the executed path.
  03-ir.md:79  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Higher-order primitives: `Op::Scan`] **What Phase 2 built, and what's still deferred.** Phase 2 built the `early_exit` mechanism (above), BPTT differentiability through a scan body (unroll-then-differentiate pre-pass — see [04-optimization](04-optimization.md)), and the first non-SSM consumer (Modern Hopfield associative-memory retrieval, `hopfield_retrieve`). Still deferred: multi-carry; an `Op::Scan` native kernel (kept out on purpose — it would turn the slot-1/`last_state` OOB into a live silent read); implicit-function-theorem / deep-equilibrium gradients (BPTT is truncated to the static `bound`); a symbolic `bound`.
  03-ir.md:85  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Storage classes and sessions] **Shared** (weights/constants — `Op::Const`): one storage for the whole model, identical across all sessions; side-table keyed by `NodeId`. Loaded from the native file; written back only when it changes (e.g. training), at checkpoints.
  03-ir.md:87  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Storage classes and sessions] **Transient** (activations — everything else): ephemeral scratch, allocated per realize and freed after use. Never persisted, never returned to the file. A transient value **does** cross devices mid-realize on a multi-device path (a D2D `Op::Copy`), but never crosses to disk.
  03-ir.md:103  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Data-dependent output shapes] **Default — capacity shape + valid-count.** The node's shape is a static **upper bound** (capacity); a second output carries the true count (via the multi-output bundle, [12-multi-output](12-multi-output.md)). Downstream ops honor the count as a host-scalar param — the same pattern the KV-cache uses with `cached_len`. Node shapes stay concrete; planning stays static.
  03-ir.md:104  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Data-dependent output shapes] **Fallback — symbolic dim + realize-time specialization.** Where worst-case capacity is too wasteful, a bounded symbolic dim is specialized at the realize barrier among a few pre-planned size buckets. Heavier; used only when capacity waste is measured to matter.
  03-ir.md:118  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Tolerance metadata] **Budgets are propagated, not embedded.** The optimizer reads the hierarchical specification (graph default → subgraph → per-op) when it needs a candidate rewrite's budget.
  03-ir.md:119  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Tolerance metadata] **Per-call overrides don't mutate the IR.** A `realize(tolerance: ...)` override is communicated to the route picker, not written into the graph. The graph's tolerance is the author's intent; per-call overrides are the caller's adaptation.
  03-ir.md:119  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > Tolerance metadata] Per-call overrides don't mutate the IR.
  03-ir.md:129  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > The base map: fully-decomposed primitive DAG, permanently retained] retained as a permanent artifact
  03-ir.md:131  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > The base map: fully-decomposed primitive DAG, permanently retained] **Re-optimization from canonical state.** When telemetry changes (new device, memory pressure, refined profile data), re-optimizing from the base map is straightforward — it's the canonical input. Re-optimizing from the optimized graph would require undoing fusions and placements first.
  03-ir.md:139  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > The optimized form: the multi-path graph (the plan IS the graph)] multiple alternative routes that diverge and reconverge
  03-ir.md:143  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > The optimized form: the multi-path graph (the plan IS the graph)] **Alternatives are paths, not a per-node side-table.** A region with two viable implementations (a CPU path and a CUDA path; a fused vs unfused form) appears as two sub-paths that branch from a common node and reconverge at a later common node. The points where paths diverge are **decision points** (branch points); everything between two decision points is a fixed **run** that dispatches as a unit. Decision points are **few** — most of the graph is a single agreed run — which is what lets the runtime decide rarely and dispatch long sequences rather than choosing per op (see [06-runtime](06-runtime.md)).
  03-ir.md:147  [Directive / StatusNote] [Intermediate representation > The optimized form: the multi-path graph (the plan IS the graph)] **The surviving paths are a bounded Pareto frontier, per ending device.** `optimize_graph` does not keep every alternative — that explodes combinatorially (the 2026-06-14 decisions-log entry records the prototype that demonstrated this). It keeps, per ending device, the Pareto-optimal paths over the ranked axes — **one central time metric, per-tier memory, precision, accuracy** — which stays naturally small (order 10²) because those axes are kept low-dimensional (a single time metric, memory as tiers, discrete precision/accuracy). A **crowding-distance cap (keep-per-device)** is the hard backstop. The cap is **per ending device** (up to `keep` each, crowding-reduced) — never a single N across all devices, which would strand slow devices (the scalar-top-N failure mode). So ≥1 path per device survives automatically, and the total is ≤ `keep` × devices: on a 5-device machine with a fast-but-memory-heavy and a slow-but-light kernel per device, ~2 Pareto-optimal paths per device (~10 total) is the intended outcome — not "3 total." Multiple paths survive per device deliberately: a fast-but-memory-heavy path and a slow-but-light one are both kept (the runtime picks by current memory pressure), and a path may span devices (so two paths colocated at one node are *not* mergeable). Convergence merges two paths only when they are **forward-identical** from a point on; never strand the last path for a (device, backend).
  03-ir.md:147  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > The optimized form: the multi-path graph (the plan IS the graph)] per ending device
  03-ir.md:162  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > How the user-facing form, base map, and optimized graph relate] The **user-facing form** is what Tensor ops or an import produce.
  03-ir.md:172  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Persisting the unified graph (base map + optimized paths)] **Local reload is fast via scoped re-optimization.** `map_from_file` loads the whole graph. The optimized paths are tagged with the hardware/backend assumptions they were built under; on load the runtime validates them — do their kernels still resolve (by revision-hash)? have devices/backends changed? — and runs **only localized re-optimization** for what changed (a device added, a backend updated, an invalidated path), reusing every still-valid path. Same machine, unchanged backends → nearly free.
  03-ir.md:172  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > Persisting the unified graph (base map + optimized paths)] Local reload is fast via scoped re-optimization.
  03-ir.md:172  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > Persisting the unified graph (base map + optimized paths)] only localized re-optimization
  03-ir.md:173  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > Persisting the unified graph (base map + optimized paths)] **It works anywhere, even unstripped.** Shipping a `.fuel` carrying another machine's optimized paths is harmless: foreign hardware fails the validation, those paths drop, and re-optimization rebuilds from the always-portable base map. The only cost is a little extra file size.
  03-ir.md:182  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > What lives where: a quick reference] Op enum (primitives)
  03-ir.md:183  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > What lives where: a quick reference] Fused-op registry (`fn`-pointer entries)
  03-ir.md:184  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > What lives where: a quick reference] Fused-op identities (declarative, Tier 2)
  03-ir.md:187  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > What lives where: a quick reference] Base map (portable, L1)
  03-ir.md:189  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > What lives where: a quick reference] Node storage class
  03-ir.md:190  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > What lives where: a quick reference] Shared storage (weights)
  03-ir.md:191  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > What lives where: a quick reference] Session storage (KV etc.)
  03-ir.md:192  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > What lives where: a quick reference] Transient storage (activations)
  03-ir.md:193  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > What lives where: a quick reference] Layout side-table
  03-ir.md:194  [Named / Description] [Intermediate representation > What lives where: a quick reference] Tolerance metadata
  03-ir.md:203  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > See also] [01-identity](01-identity.md) — why DAG-as-IR is the central commitment.
  03-ir.md:205  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > See also] [06-runtime](06-runtime.md) — sequence dispatch, the runtime selector picking among paths at decision points, background re-optimization.
  03-ir.md:207  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > See also] [11-persistence](11-persistence.md) — base map (portable) vs optimized graph (per-hardware); storage write-back rules.
  03-ir.md:208  [Directive / Description] [Intermediate representation > See also] [14-lifecycle](14-lifecycle.md) — the end-to-end flow this section's structures move through.
  04-optimization.md:3  [Named / Description] [Optimization] v0.10 records the BPTT-via-decompose backward path
  04-optimization.md:3  [Named / Description] [Optimization] no bespoke `*_BACKWARD` fused op
  04-optimization.md:3  [Named / Description] [Optimization] v0.9 gives the DecompositionMap a `Scan` terminal entry
  04-optimization.md:3  [Named / Description] [Optimization] no `LoweringRule`
  04-optimization.md:3  [Named / Description] [Optimization] v0.8 adds the fused-op Layer-1 *cost-from-decompose* default
  04-optimization.md:3  [Named / StatusNote] [Optimization] v0.6 adds the 2026-06-20 adaptive-runtime-fusion decision
  04-optimization.md:3  [Named / Description] [Optimization] `decompose` totality + never-panic + primitive→self
  04-optimization.md:15  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > The two-stage transformation] **Decomposition.** User-facing form → base map. Every `Op::Fused(id, params)` node is replaced by its primitive decomposition, recursively. Output: a graph containing only primitive `Op` variants — no `Op::Fused` arms. Deterministic, one-shot, retained as a permanent artifact (see [03-ir](03-ir.md#the-base-map-fully-decomposed-primitive-dag-permanently-retained)).
  04-optimization.md:24  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > DecompositionMap] **Keys**: `FusedOpId` (registry-assigned identifiers from [03-ir](03-ir.md)). Every fused-op entry in the registry contributes one key.
  04-optimization.md:25  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > DecompositionMap] **Values**: a decomposition function `fn(graph, node_id, params) -> NodeId` that, given a fused-op node, appends its primitive subgraph to the graph and returns the subgraph's output node.
  04-optimization.md:40  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > DecompositionMap > `decompose` is total, never-panics, and is the base map's fixpoint] The **base map is the fixpoint of `decompose` over every node** (lower until `decompose(x) == x` everywhere) — exactly this section's `optimize_graph` rewrite-to-fixpoint model.
  04-optimization.md:51  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > OptimizationMap] **Matcher**: examines a node and its surrounding subgraph; returns `Some(match)` if the rule applies, `None` otherwise. Matches identify the specific subgraph instance that will be rewritten.
  04-optimization.md:52  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > OptimizationMap] **Rewriter**: given a match, emits the replacement subgraph and records the consumer-edge remapping so the optimizer can rewire downstream nodes.
  04-optimization.md:64  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > OptimizationMap] **Family**: lowering (high-level → primitive subgraph; runs in decomposition), fusion (primitive subgraph → high-level; runs in optimization), algebraic (primitive ↔ primitive equivalence-preserving rewrite), tolerance-gated (rewrite that requires non-zero tolerance budget).
  04-optimization.md:66  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > OptimizationMap] **Frontier compatibility**: whether this rule can fire safely while concurrent optimize-and-execute is in progress (see [the sliding window section](#the-sliding-window-optimization-and-execution-overlap) below). Two values:
  04-optimization.md:82  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > Bounding the frontier: Pareto per device + crowding cap] **The right axes keep the natural frontier small and lossless.** The optimizer keeps, **per ending device**, the Pareto-optimal paths over the ranker vector. Because the rankers are kept low-dimensional — **one central time metric** (not a min/max pair), **memory as discrete tiers** (not continuous bytes), **discrete precision/accuracy levels** — the Pareto frontier stays naturally small (order 10² across a deep model) and is **lossless**: a path dominated on the same ending device can never beat its dominator downstream (futures are identical, costs additive/monotone), so dropping it loses no reachable optimum. In the prototype, this alone held the frontier flat at ~100 paths over 128 regions with no cap.
  04-optimization.md:82  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > Bounding the frontier: Pareto per device + crowding cap] per ending device
  04-optimization.md:84  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > Bounding the frontier: Pareto per device + crowding cap] **A crowding-distance cap is the hard backstop.** When a device bucket's frontier exceeds a configured `keep`, it is reduced NSGA-II-style by crowding distance — retaining the per-axis extremes and the best frontier-*spanners*, **not** the top-N on a single metric (which would discard whole tradeoff dimensions). The prototype found `keep ≈ 32/device` matches the no-cap optimum on every runtime query (fastest / most-precise / least-memory / balanced) and bounds even adversarial continuous-axis cases.
  04-optimization.md:84  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > Bounding the frontier: Pareto per device + crowding cap] A crowding-distance cap is the hard backstop.
  04-optimization.md:86  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > Bounding the frontier: Pareto per device + crowding cap] a path may span devices
  04-optimization.md:92  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > What counts as a decision point] **Kernel-variant choices**: same op, different kernel implementations (e.g., cuBLAS vs custom matmul; different tile shapes; different precision-tradeoff variants for the same backend).
  04-optimization.md:93  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > What counts as a decision point] **Placement choices**: same op, different (backend, device) assignments.
  04-optimization.md:94  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > What counts as a decision point] **Fusion-vs-decomposition choices**: a subgraph that can be left as primitives OR fused into a single registered fused op.
  04-optimization.md:95  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > What counts as a decision point] **Algebraic-rewrite choices**: a subgraph that has equivalent algebraic forms with different op counts or operand traffic.
  04-optimization.md:96  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > What counts as a decision point] **Tolerance-trade-off choices**: a region that can run strict OR with a non-zero tolerance budget for a faster approximate variant.
  04-optimization.md:134  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Per-decision-point alternatives > The tunable: `keep` per device (not a per-decision-point N)] `keep` — the crowding-cap size per ending device
  04-optimization.md:149  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] **Frontier advance is monotonic.** The frontier never moves backward. Once a region is finalized, no future optimization can touch it. This is what makes execution-eligible-from-frontier safe: an executing node knows its op, kernel, inputs, and outputs are bit-stable.
  04-optimization.md:149  [Named / Description] [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] Frontier advance is monotonic.
  04-optimization.md:150  [Directive / StatusNote] [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] **`Concurrent`-classified rules only fire ahead of the frontier.** A `Concurrent` rule whose match would touch a finalized node is deferred; if the node has already executed by the time the rule's match is considered, the match is rejected (the node has the value it had; rewriting it is meaningless). The classification's contract is that this rejection is rare — `Concurrent` rules are designed to operate within the optimizer's lookahead window where the frontier hasn't caught up yet.
  04-optimization.md:152  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] **Concurrent and whole-graph alternatives coexist at decision points.** Within one decision point, some alternatives may have been produced exclusively by `Concurrent` rules (concurrent-compatible) and others may include `WholeGraph` rule applications (concurrent-incompatible). The picker reads per-realize concurrency policy to select among compatible alternatives at each decision point.
  04-optimization.md:153  [Named / Description] [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] Per-decision-point commit at the frontier.
  04-optimization.md:157  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] `Concurrency::Auto` (default) — picker prefers concurrent-compatible alternatives when they're competitive on cost. Falls back to whole-graph alternatives only if concurrent ones are demonstrably worse.
  04-optimization.md:163  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] **Latency to first output is bounded by execution time of the early portion of the graph, not by optimization time of the whole graph.** For large models, optimization of the deepest layers can run in parallel with execution of the shallowest. Meaningful for inference TTFT.
  04-optimization.md:164  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] **Graphs that mutate (autoregressive decoding extending the same graph repeatedly) can have execution and optimization interleaved naturally.** As the user appends nodes for the next decode step, the optimizer works on them; nodes from previous steps are already executing or done.
  04-optimization.md:168  [Named / Description] [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] Implementation complexity is high.
  04-optimization.md:169  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] **Cost-model accuracy matters more.** When the frontier advances, the optimizer commits irreversibly. Bad cost estimates → bad commits. Empirical Judge data is more important under concurrent execution than under whole-graph execution, where errors could be corrected before execution starts.
  04-optimization.md:170  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > The sliding window: optimization and execution overlap] **Some optimization opportunities only show up in `WholeGraph` rules.** A workload that benefits substantially from cross-graph CSE or global re-association will favor whole-graph routes; concurrent execution leaves those wins on the table. The optimizer can produce both kinds and let the route picker compare costs honestly.
  04-optimization.md:219  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Load-time incremental planning + cost-based placement (v0.4) > Cost-based placement: the carry-forward DP] **State**: for each frontier node, `best[d]` = lowest accumulated
  04-optimization.md:224  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Load-time incremental planning + cost-based placement (v0.4) > Cost-based placement: the carry-forward DP] **Extension**: when node N arrives, for each device `d`:
  04-optimization.md:230  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Load-time incremental planning + cost-based placement (v0.4) > Cost-based placement: the carry-forward DP] **Fused jumps**: fusion candidates enter the same recurrence as
  04-optimization.md:230  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Load-time incremental planning + cost-based placement (v0.4) > Cost-based placement: the carry-forward DP] Fused jumps
  04-optimization.md:262  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Load-time incremental planning + cost-based placement (v0.4) > Plan-fragment memoization (repeated structure)] Persisted-plan cache
  04-optimization.md:266  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Load-time incremental planning + cost-based placement (v0.4) > Plan-fragment memoization (repeated structure)] Record-once-replay execution
  04-optimization.md:303  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Rankers and the cost model: a per-path cost vector, ranked] only for the sentinel
  04-optimization.md:303  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Rankers and the cost model: a per-path cost vector, ranked] measured › declared › composed-from-recipe › (never) zero
  04-optimization.md:319  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Precision-filter pass: runs before cost ranking] **The user's per-call precision requirement.** If the user has specified a precision floor (e.g., "≤ 0.1% relative error per element"), alternatives whose `max_relative` exceeds it are pruned regardless of how cheap they are.
  04-optimization.md:324  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Precision-filter pass: runs before cost ranking] Repeatability is a separate opt-in
  04-optimization.md:357  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Relationship to PR 3's existing rule registry] **PR 3's `Rule` trait extends to declarative + callable engines uniformly.** Today PR 3's rules are all callable. The architecture's commitment is that the trait can host either kind without re-design.
  04-optimization.md:358  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Relationship to PR 3's existing rule registry] **Decomposition rules are PR 3's lowering family.** Today PR 3's `SoftmaxLastDimLowerRule` is hand-written; under the FusedOpRegistry refactor, it's auto-generated from the registry entry's decomposition function. The mechanics are unchanged.
  04-optimization.md:358  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Relationship to PR 3's existing rule registry] Decomposition rules are PR 3's lowering family.
  04-optimization.md:359  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Relationship to PR 3's existing rule registry] **OptimizationMap rules are PR 3's fusion family + new algebraic-rewrite rules + tolerance-gated rules.** Today PR 3's `SoftmaxLastDimFuseRule` is the only fusion rule; the architecture admits dozens more, plus algebraic rewrites that aren't fusion at all.
  04-optimization.md:360  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Relationship to PR 3's existing rule registry] **Per-decision-point alternatives are now landed.** The driver tracks the bounded per-device Pareto frontier at each location and records its surviving paths as `Op::Branch` decision points on the optimized graph — the per-device frontier + crowding cap replaced PR 3's first-match-wins single transformation. The `Rule` trait surface is unchanged, as committed.
  04-optimization.md:360  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Relationship to PR 3's existing rule registry] Per-decision-point alternatives are now landed.
  04-optimization.md:361  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > Relationship to PR 3's existing rule registry] **Concurrent optimize-and-execute is not yet landed.** Production optimization runs synchronously to completion inside the realize bridge, then execution proceeds; the sliding-window model (frontier tracking + atomic-swap commit) remains the destination. The runtime *route picker* that selects an arm per branch by live telemetry **is** landed (it resolves the surviving paths at dispatch time); what remains is the monotonic optimization frontier that lets optimization and execution overlap.
  04-optimization.md:361  [Named / Description] [Optimization > Relationship to PR 3's existing rule registry] Concurrent optimize-and-execute is not yet landed.
  04-optimization.md:369  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > What this rules out] **No e-graph saturation on the per-realize hot path.** E-graphs (egg, equality saturation) are unsuitable for hot-path optimization. But the offline multi-path path-search `optimize_graph` itself performs is e-graph-*adjacent*, and that is **in-bounds**: `optimize_graph` runs at load/import, not per realize. E-graph techniques may also feed the OptimizationMap as an offline rule-discovery tool. What stays out is e-graph saturation *on the per-realize hot path* (see [09-non-goals](09-non-goals.md)).
  04-optimization.md:369  [Named / Description] [Optimization > What this rules out] No e-graph saturation on the per-realize hot path.
  04-optimization.md:371  [Directive / StatusNote] [Optimization > What this rules out] **No *untrusted* user-installable rules at runtime.** Letting an end user hot-load arbitrary optimization code introduces a security and stability surface fuel doesn't take on (see [09-non-goals](09-non-goals.md)). **This bounds trust + provenance, not "runtime" per se** (reconciled 2026-06-20, [10-decisions-log](10-decisions-log.md)): the OptimizationMap is populated at startup for *untrusted* sources, but **trusted, Fuel-orchestrated, cost-gated runtime registration of new fused-op identities** (Tier 2) is in-bounds. There, Fuel chooses the sub-base-map region to fuse (strategy stays in the optimizer), a trusted backend synthesizes the kernel, the result arrives as a **declarative recipe** (`decompose` + `pattern` over existing primitives + an FKC `PrecisionGuarantee`), and the route picker cost-gates adoption — no untrusted code, no new primitive. The implementation mechanism is the declarative-pattern engine (today stubbed, `PatternKind::Declarative => false` at `fuel-graph/src/opt.rs`), which is the prerequisite for that runtime registration. The kernel binding table (implementations, distinct from rules) is already runtime-extensible (`extend_global_bindings`).
  04-optimization.md:371  [Named / Description] [Optimization > What this rules out] No *untrusted* user-installable rules at runtime.
  04-optimization.md:372  [Directive / Description] [Optimization > What this rules out] **No global optimization passes that aren't rule-based.** Every transformation goes through the rule machinery. "Special pass that's not a rule" is forbidden because it makes the optimizer's behavior unanalyzable and its commits unreproducible.
  04-optimization.md:372  [Named / Description] [Optimization > What this rules out] No global optimization passes that aren't rule-based.

  06-runtime.md:3  [Named / StatusNote] [Runtime] v1.5 also reconciles to the 2026-06-20 adaptive-runtime-fusion decision
  06-runtime.md:3  [Named / Description] [Runtime] v1.3 refines landed-vs-intent and salvages two durable facts
  06-runtime.md:3  [Named / Description] [Runtime] few decision points (branch points)
  06-runtime.md:15  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > The runtime's responsibilities] **Path resolution at decision points.** At the few decision points (branch points) of the multi-path graph, the runtime selector picks among the surviving per-device Pareto paths by current telemetry (device load, per-tier free memory). Between decision points the path is fixed.
  06-runtime.md:18  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > The runtime's responsibilities] **Synchronization at join points.** Waiting on outputs from multiple parallel branches before a join op.
  06-runtime.md:22  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > The runtime's responsibilities] **Strategic decisions** (placement, fusion, kernel-variant choice) — the optimizer's. The runtime executes the path the picker chooses.
  06-runtime.md:45  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Route picker (the runtime selector / "Picker 2")] live per-tier free memory
  06-runtime.md:45  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Route picker (the runtime selector / "Picker 2")] The pick now runs inside the executor
  06-runtime.md:45  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Route picker (the runtime selector / "Picker 2")] the architecture's intent but not yet built
  06-runtime.md:45  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Route picker (the runtime selector / "Picker 2")] per-decision-point during the dispatch walk
  06-runtime.md:67  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > Dispatch: runs, not single ops] **Shallow (just-in-time)**: commit the next run only as a slot frees. Maximum adaptivity; risk of backend idle while the picker thinks. For very small runs where decision overhead dominates.
  06-runtime.md:68  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > Dispatch: runs, not single ops] **Deep**: queue many runs ahead. Backends always busy; risk that telemetry has shifted by execute-time. For very large runs where execution dominates.
  06-runtime.md:69  [Directive / StatusNote] [Runtime > Dispatch: runs, not single ops] **Bounded (default)**: keep exactly enough runs queued to fill currently-available slots; queue more only as one finishes. Balances adaptivity vs throughput — backends never idle, decisions stay current. The bound = sum of backends' currently-available slot counts.
  06-runtime.md:73  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Dispatch: runs, not single ops > Cancellation is not supported] dispatched runs are committed.
  06-runtime.md:79  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > Data parallelism: independent runs run concurrently] The ready-set tracks all runs whose inputs are available and frontier-finalized.
  06-runtime.md:80  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > Data parallelism: independent runs run concurrently] Each ready run is dispatched to an available slot on its assigned backend.
  06-runtime.md:109  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > Cross-tier prefetch: the plan is the schedule] **Disk → host RAM** (larger-than-RAM): the plan issues `madvise(WILLNEED)` / page-touch for the mmap'd weights of upcoming runs ahead of the execution frontier, so cold pages fault in while earlier runs execute. Without this, an out-of-core model thrashes; with it, it streams.
  06-runtime.md:109  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Cross-tier prefetch: the plan is the schedule] Disk → host RAM
  06-runtime.md:110  [Directive / StatusNote] [Runtime > Cross-tier prefetch: the plan is the schedule] **Host RAM → device VRAM** (larger-than-VRAM): the plan issues H2D for upcoming runs ahead of the frontier and evicts (Move/Release) device-resident buffers a later run no longer needs, bounded by the device's free-memory budget from `BackendRuntime`.
  06-runtime.md:110  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Cross-tier prefetch: the plan is the schedule] Host RAM → device VRAM
  06-runtime.md:116  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Background re-optimization] per decision point with merged path sets
  06-runtime.md:118  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > Background re-optimization] The loaded multi-path graph is the working state.
  06-runtime.md:120  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Background re-optimization] keep the per-device Pareto frontier (crowding-capped)
  06-runtime.md:137  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Scoped re-optimization] Backend kernel-revision hash changed
  06-runtime.md:138  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Scoped re-optimization] Profile data refined for cells `(op, dtype, size_class, backend, device)`
  06-runtime.md:139  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Scoped re-optimization] Tolerance configuration changed
  06-runtime.md:141  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Scoped re-optimization] Loading a `.fuel` on changed hardware
  06-runtime.md:147  [Named / Description] [Runtime > Local Judge baseline: bundled in-package] ships a bundled baseline Judge dataset in-package
  06-runtime.md:159  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > What the runtime persists] **The cached resolved route** (from the picker's telemetry-caching). Reused while telemetry is stable.
  06-runtime.md:160  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > What the runtime persists] **The persisted graph / optimization cache** (per [11-persistence](11-persistence.md)). **Memory-mapped at startup**, not read into memory: only the header and the decision-point index are touched before the first realize; node-data pages load on first access via the OS page cache; pages for never-taken paths may never load. Cache files are mmap-friendly (relative offsets, no process-absolute pointers).
  06-runtime.md:160  [Named / Description] [Runtime > What the runtime persists] Memory-mapped at startup
  06-runtime.md:164  [Named / Description] [Runtime > What the runtime persists > Kernel resolution: optional pre-resolve, else lazy] lazily
  06-runtime.md:168  [Named / Description] [Runtime > What the runtime persists > Cache updates: write-new-file-and-swap] write-new-file-and-swap
  06-runtime.md:176  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > What this rules out] **No runtime kernel selection across paths the optimizer didn't keep.** If a variant isn't in the surviving per-device frontier, the runtime can't reach it. The optimizer surfaces competitive paths; the runtime picks among them.
  06-runtime.md:177  [Directive / StatusNote] [Runtime > What this rules out] **No silent runtime fallback to a different op.** A chosen kernel failing (OOM, hardware fault) surfaces the error; the runtime doesn't transparently switch paths without telling the user. (A future "fallback path" feature could register runtime-fallbacks, but not v1.) **What this does *not* rule out** (reconciled 2026-06-20, [10-decisions-log](10-decisions-log.md) G4): **Fuel-orchestrated runtime kernel registration** is a v1 reality, not a post-v1 feature. The kernel **binding table is already runtime-extensible** (`extend_global_bindings` — append-only, multi-sibling, with route-cache invalidation; Tier 1), so JIT-ing a kernel for an **existing op identity** and ingesting it lands today; registering a **new fused-op identity** (Tier 2) is the in-flight goal, append-only with stable ids. The genuine non-goal is narrower than "runtime registration" — it is **silent op *substitution* mid-realize**: swapping the *math* a chosen path computes without the optimizer's cost-gated, telemetry-visible decision. JIT-registered kernels are cost-gated by the route picker and surface through telemetry; a kernel that doesn't win is never used.
  06-runtime.md:177  [Named / Description] [Runtime > What this rules out] No silent runtime fallback to a different op.
  06-runtime.md:177  [Named / Description] [Runtime > What this rules out] binding table is already runtime-extensible
  06-runtime.md:177  [Named / Description] [Runtime > What this rules out] silent op *substitution* mid-realize
  06-runtime.md:178  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > What this rules out] **No graph rebuild per input or per token.** The graph is the input-independent model (per [03-ir](03-ir.md)); autoregressive decode **reuses** the persistent decode-step graph with advancing per-session state, it does not rebuild or extend the graph each token. Structural changes happen at load/import + optimize, not mid-loop.
  06-runtime.md:178  [Named / Description] [Runtime > What this rules out] No graph rebuild per input or per token.
  06-runtime.md:180  [Named / Description] [Runtime > What this rules out] no dedicated mask op
  06-runtime.md:180  [Named / Description] [Runtime > What this rules out] re-bound each pass
  06-runtime.md:181  [Directive / Description] [Runtime > What this rules out] **Within-realize observations don't change the current realize.** Online measurements (the expected-vs-real dispatch check) feed **background re-optimization** for *subsequent* realizes; the in-flight realize runs on the route it started with.
  06-runtime.md:181  [Named / Description] [Runtime > What this rules out] Within-realize observations don't change the current realize.

  10-decisions-log.md:79  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Five competitive edges.** Cross-backend placement aware of fusion catalogs; algebraic-equivalence rewrites; top-N route preservation; pattern-harvest-driven fused-op development; per-op tolerance budgets. (01-identity.)
  10-decisions-log.md:81  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **The bet stated as compounding.** "Bigger fused kernels matter and will keep mattering. Above the kernel layer, optimization techniques that span ops, span backends, adapt at runtime, and trade controlled error for compute will keep finding wins backend-internal fusion can't reach." Both layers compound. (01-identity.)
  10-decisions-log.md:83  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Op-shape A: closed enum primitives + `Op::Fused(id, params)` arm.** No separate `NodeKind` discriminator type. After exploring four options (A two-tier; B newtype + collapsed registry; C parallel types with NodeKind; D flat enum with all ops), the user chose A for long-term goals — preserves open-registry property for fused ops while keeping the type system reflective of the architecture. (03-ir.)
  10-decisions-log.md:87  [Directive / StatusNote] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Three coexisting artifacts**: user-facing form, base map (canonical primitive DAG, retained as permanent artifact), optimized form (top-N alternatives per decision point with pre-resolved kernels). (03-ir.) *(**Amended 2026-06-14** by the "plan is the graph" redirection below: the optimized form is not a separate top-N artifact — it is the **same graph transformed in place** into bounded Pareto paths + decision points. User-facing form and base map stand; "top-N alternatives" is superseded by the per-device frontier. See [03-ir](03-ir.md).)*
  10-decisions-log.md:87  [Named / StatusNote] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] Amended 2026-06-14
  10-decisions-log.md:89  [Directive / StatusNote] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Per-decision-point alternatives, not N global routes.** The optimizer preserves up to N alternatives per decision point (default N=3); decisions can be coupled via conditional cost adjustments. The runtime route picker resolves alternatives at dispatch time using current telemetry, mixing and matching across decision points. Strictly more flexible than top-N complete plans. (04-optimization.) *(**Superseded 2026-06-14** by the "plan is the graph" redirection below: alternatives attach to **branch points** and the retained paths per device are bounded by a **Pareto frontier + crowding cap**, not a fixed N. The "per decision point, not N global routes" direction stands; the fixed `N=3` does not. See [04-optimization](04-optimization.md) and item 6's superseded note.)*
  10-decisions-log.md:91  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Three forms of parallelism named explicitly**: pipeline (sliding window between optimizer and executor), data (independent subgraphs run concurrently), within-kernel (SIMD/intra-kernel concurrency owned by backends). (04-optimization, 05-backend-contract, 06-runtime.)
  10-decisions-log.md:93  [Directive / StatusNote] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Slot-capacity advertisement model.** Even same-device parallelism flows from DAG-level decisions, not backend-internal policy. Backends advertise total + currently-available slots; runtime allocates work to slots. Honors the "backends advertise; they don't decide" principle even at same-device granularity. (05-backend-contract, 06-runtime.)
  10-decisions-log.md:95  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Concurrent execute is per-rule, per-route.** Rules self-declare frontier-compatibility (`Concurrent` | `WholeGraph`); routes built from `Concurrent` rules can execute concurrently with optimization; routes including `WholeGraph` rules require whole-graph optimization first. Per-realize concurrency policy: `Auto` / `Required` / `Forbidden`. (04-optimization.)
  10-decisions-log.md:97  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Tolerance as fifth competitive edge with hierarchical specification.** `Strict` | `Relative(x)` | `Absolute(x)`; graph-default → subgraph override → per-op override → per-call override; tightest-wins composition. Best-effort upper bounds annotations now → empirical Judge measurement later. (07-tolerance.)
  10-decisions-log.md:99  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Tolerance discovery and calibration.** Opt-in workflow that runs models through user-supplied test inputs to discover maximum acceptable per-op tolerance budgets. Metrics library (accuracy, KL, perplexity, embedding distance, custom); search algorithms (greedy, sensitivity-first, Bayesian); hierarchical granularity (per-region → per-layer → per-op); discovered recipes shareable on community server. (07-tolerance.)
  10-decisions-log.md:101  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Reference backend dissolved into per-kernel `PrecisionGuarantee` structure.** Each kernel declares `{bit_stable_on_same_hardware, max_ulp, max_relative, max_absolute, notes}`. The always-built backend (fuel-cpu-backend by convention) commits to providing at least one `bit_stable` kernel for every primitive op as the coverage guarantee. Cleaner than special-status backend. (05-backend-contract.)
  10-decisions-log.md:103  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Pattern harvest opt-in with first-use prompt.** Industry-standard pattern (Homebrew analytics prompt, rustup installer, mise first-run) captures "people who don't care" segment without going to silent opt-out. "No silent telemetry" added as explicit non-goal. (08-pattern-harvest, 09-non-goals.)
  10-decisions-log.md:105  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Four-flow community telemetry infrastructure.** One opt-in pipeline carries: pattern harvest (op sequences) + tolerance recipes (per-op error budgets) + hardware fingerprints (auto-populates target sets) + kernel-stat summaries (refines static cost annotations toward measured reality). Per-flow privacy commitments documented. (08-pattern-harvest.)
  10-decisions-log.md:109  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Remote loader integration.** `fuel.load("hf://...")`, `github://`, `https://`; auto-discovers sibling cache and tolerance-recipe artifacts at the model's location. fuel-loaders is the implementing crate. (02-layers, 11-persistence.)
  10-decisions-log.md:111  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Multi-version DAG-format support + opportunistic migration.** Newer fuel reads at least the previous N format versions; format additions are backward-compatible where feasible; background re-optimization migrates older caches to the current format as a side effect of producing refined plans. (11-persistence, 06-runtime.)
  10-decisions-log.md:113  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Background re-optimization with per-decision-point atomic swap.** Downloaded caches are starting points; background optimization with local empirical Judge data refines them via merge logic (cached alternatives + newly-discovered alternatives, re-ranked by local cost, top N retained per point). Atomic swap is per-decision-point, not whole-DAG. Same primitive used by concurrent optimize-and-execute. (06-runtime.)
  10-decisions-log.md:115  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Local Judge baseline initialization.** The Judge can optionally start from a community-aggregated profile for the user's hardware fingerprint before refining with local measurements. (06-runtime.)
  10-decisions-log.md:117  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Precision-filter pass before cost ranking.** Alternatives at each decision point are filtered by their `PrecisionGuarantee` against the user's per-call precision requirement and cumulative tolerance budget; cost ranking ranks the survivors. (04-optimization.)
  10-decisions-log.md:117  [Named / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] Precision-filter pass before cost ranking.
  10-decisions-log.md:119  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **Scoped re-optimization based on trigger.** Each decision point records its dependencies (kernels, devices, profile cells); triggers compute the affected scope by intersection. Most triggers (device removed, kernel updated, profile data refined) affect a small subset of decision points, not the whole graph. (06-runtime, 11-persistence.)
  10-decisions-log.md:121  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-05-09 — Architecture set v1.0 established] **mmap'd cache with lazy KernelRef resolution.** Cache files are mmap'd at process startup, not read into memory; only the cache header gets touched immediately. KernelRef resolution is lazy at decision-point pick time. Startup is near-instant for cache hits. Write-new-file-and-swap on cache update; mmap-fallback to read-into-memory where mmap is unavailable. (06-runtime, 11-persistence.)

The decisions log (this file) records material architectural changes going forward. (00-index, 10-decisions-log.)

  10-decisions-log.md:240  [Directive / StatusNote] [Decisions log > 2026-06-08 — Runtime-snapshot persistence artifact (L3); save-all-activations rejected] *Merge model + plan + snapshot into one file.* Rejected — divergent lifecycles (weights shared everywhere, plan hardware-dependent, snapshot run-dependent); merging forces re-shipping weights when the plan changes and over-invalidates.   10-decisions-log.md:241  [Directive / StatusNote] [Decisions log > 2026-06-08 — Runtime-snapshot persistence artifact (L3); save-all-activations rejected] *Drop the snapshot concept (leave durable state to app code).* Rejected — KV-cache / optimizer-state checkpointing is a real serving/training need; a defined artifact + invalidation is worth the architecture line.
  10-decisions-log.md:303  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] **Multi-path, decisions at branches.** Alternatives are real divergent/reconverging paths; decision points = branch points (few), not per-node. The built "every kernel-bearing node gets an AlternativeSet, resolved per node" over-generalized "decision point" and is the drift to undo.
  10-decisions-log.md:306  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] **Finalize-to-disk is the default run mode, not a precondition.** Any in-memory model can be finalized to an mmap'd file — yielding fast reload, snapshotting, training-persistence, AND larger-than-RAM in one mechanism — but the base map runs before finalize. Write-back at explicit checkpoints (`msync`), never write-through-per-step.
  10-decisions-log.md:308  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] **`optimize_graph` = pathfinders + rankers + optimizers, run lock-step.** Pathfinders add candidate paths (algebraic reshaping; dependency tracking for parallelism; windowed scanner over fused + primitive ops). Rankers measure each path: **timing** (the Judge — see #8), **precision** (digits), **accuracy** (ULP/rounding/monotonicity), **memory** (per-tier vector, see #10). Optimizers merge/discard sub-optimal paths (per-op tolerance; duplicate-path convergence; path-timing) and never strand the last path for a (device,backend) nor touch a path in an active inference/training cycle. Tie-break order: precision → accuracy → memory.
  10-decisions-log.md:309  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] **Bounding the path explosion (prototype-validated).** Naive per-device Pareto pruning EXPLODES (frontier passes 3000 by region 14 of 128). The bound comes from two complementary things: **(a) the right axes** — ONE central time metric (median/average for throughput, p99 for latency; **drop `t_min` as a selection axis** — "fastest best-case" is not a selection goal; the Judge measures the full distribution but the optimizer optimizes on one mode-selected metric), memory as discrete **tiers**, precision/accuracy as discrete levels → a naturally small (~100), FLAT, **lossless** frontier with no cap; and **(b) a crowding-distance-capped beam** (NSGA-II style, per ending-device) as a hard backstop — keep≈32/device matches the no-cap optimum on all tested runtime queries, and bounds even adversarial continuous-axis cases. Per-device bucketing keeps multiple paths per device (time/memory tradeoffs; multi-device-spanning paths) and never strands a device.
  10-decisions-log.md:309  [Named / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] Bounding the path explosion (prototype-validated).
  10-decisions-log.md:310  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] **Runtime picks within the surviving frontier by live telemetry** (device load, free memory tier) at branch points — this is competitive edge #3 (top-N route preservation) realized structurally. Kernel-variant choice is largely baked at optimize time; device/path choice adapts at runtime.
  10-decisions-log.md:311  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] **Three-tier memory (disk / host / device) tracked as a vector, not a scalar.** A path has separate host-RAM and device-VRAM (and disk) footprints; dominance and runtime selection are per-tier (which tier is the binding constraint decides which path wins). The plan IS the prefetch schedule across tiers: disk→RAM (mmap demand-paging + `madvise`) for larger-than-RAM, RAM→VRAM (H2D ahead of frontier) for larger-than-VRAM.
  10-decisions-log.md:312  [Named / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] `Storage` must support mmap-backed zero-copy views
  10-decisions-log.md:313  [Directive / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] **Pre-resolving kernels is an optional post-optimize step** (startup-time vs TTFT trade): resolve all `KernelRef`s up front to take the lookup off the hot path, or resolve lazily for fastest first-token.
  10-decisions-log.md:313  [Named / Description] [Decisions log > 2026-06-14 — Major redirection: "the plan IS the graph" (multi-path optimization)] Pre-resolving kernels is an optional post-optimize step
